# Master Completion Plan

> Mission started: 2026-09-09. Baseline: 28% product, 25/100 production, 10/100 autonomy. A goal is COMPLETE only after its listed acceptance criteria pass with real evidence. This file records implementation status; it is not a completion claim.

## Status and Evidence Rules

Statuses: `NOT_STARTED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `BLOCKED`, `IMPLEMENTED`, `TESTING`, `VERIFIED`, `COMPLETE`. Completion requires code integration, focused tests, integration/E2E tests where applicable, failure remediation, rerun, and code-level verification. Mocks/fixtures may test boundaries but never count as proof of a real external submission. PostgreSQL/Prisma remains authoritative; Redis/BullMQ is coordination; private S3-compatible storage holds objects; MongoDB is excluded.

## Dependency Order

`G0 → G1 → G2 → G3 → G4 → G5`; then `G6/G7 → G8 → G9 → G10 → G11 → G12 → G13 → G14/G19 → G15 → G16/G17 → G18 → G20 → G21`; `G22/G23/G24/G25/G26` build on durable execution; `G27/G28/G29/G30` are continuous gates; `G31 → G32`. Security, observability, resilience, and testing are cross-cutting and must be added with each goal, then certified in their dedicated goals.

Backup/restore drill passed into a clean database with restored user rows and `pgcrypto`, `plpgsql`, and `vector`; full `npm run check` passes (24 standard tests, 7 database tests separately), production builds pass, and dependency audit remains at zero vulnerabilities. 10. Goal 2 is COMPLETE. The API status mutation now uses one typed transition service with an explicit allowed graph, optimistic expected-version compare-and-set, actor/reason/correlation/idempotency inputs, and atomic application/history/audit/outbox writes. `CONFIRMED` requires the independent verifier and submission moves through `SUBMISSION_PENDING → UNCONFIRMED` rather than asserting success.
11. Four graph unit tests and four live PostgreSQL transaction tests pass, including idempotent replay, rollback-safe invalid transitions, stale-write denial, and a two-writer concurrency race with exactly one winner. Full validation passes with 28 standard tests, 11 live database tests separately, all seven workspace typechecks, lint, API build, and web production build. Next: Goal 3 outbox and idempotency delivery semantics.
12. Goal 3 is COMPLETE. Versioned additive migrations add outbox leases, terminal failure state, claim-ready indexes, invariant checks, service-role cross-tenant publication policy, and restricted service-role table grants. The publisher claims with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, uses expiring leases, bounded exponential retry, terminal failure accounting, and stable transport idempotency keys; a send-before-mark crash deliberately redelivers rather than losing an event.
13. Durable command and consumer idempotency now use canonical SHA-256 request identity and one PostgreSQL transaction for key reservation, side effects, and stored response. Replays return the prior response, altered requests conflict, failed commands roll back both reservation and effects, and repeated consumer deliveries execute their effect once.
14. Goal 3 validation passes: two focused unit tests; 18 live PostgreSQL tests across foundation, transition, concurrent claim, retry exhaustion, crash/redelivery, consumer dedupe, conflict, and rollback scenarios; full `npm run check` with 30 standard tests, all seven workspace typechecks, ESLint, API build, and 1,503-module web build; dependency audit remains at zero vulnerabilities. 15. Goal 4 is COMPLETE. The AutomationJob service creates versioned jobs with strict idempotent replay, serializes concurrent creation, claims by descending priority through `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, increments attempts on every lease, renews only live owner leases, completes with compare-and-set ownership, retries with delayed availability, dead-letters exhausted failures and expired final-attempt leases, and safely cancels queued or leased work.

A versioned migration tightens exact lease and terminal timestamp invariants, adds a claim-ready priority index, and permits only the restricted service role to coordinate jobs across tenants while retaining tenant isolation for the application role. 829. G18 answer-resolution hardening in the exported-but-unwired `packages/ai` question agent: two real defects were fixed. First, provenance was shape-checked but never verified, so a model could return a plausible `source` path that does not exist and launder a fabricated answer; the cited path must now resolve to real profile data and a `SAFE` answer must match it verbatim. Second, a third-party question ("What is your manager's name?", "Company Name", "Company website") could still be auto-answered from the candidate's own profile; third-party questions now fail closed to human review before any model call, and the model-output parser returns an explicit rejection reason instead of a silent null. Honest scope note: this module is exported from `@jobagent/ai` but is not imported by the API, worker, or web app, so no production path changed. The live answer path (`packages/job-engine/src/form-intelligence.ts`) was re-checked and is structurally not exposed to either defect, because it resolves only human-approved, owner- and question-identity-bound answers rather than model output. The plan previously did not mention this module at all; that omission is recorded here. Evidence: 23/23 new `question-answerer` tests covering ambiguous and third-party fields, substring false matches, malicious labels, prompt injection in form labels, injection phrasings the narrow detector deliberately does not cover, provenance fabrication, and fail-closed invariants.
830. Automation job queue-routing defect fixed: `queueForJobType` used prefix matching, so 6 of the 12 real dispatched job types routed to the wrong queue — `EXECUTE_AUTHORIZED_SUBMISSION`, `EVALUATE_ATS`, `EVALUATE_APPLICATION_QUALITY`, and both `COMPLETE_*_APPLICATION` types landed on `maintenance`, where no handler consumes them, and `RESUME_APPLICATION_AFTER_VERIFICATION` landed on `documents`. Real submission jobs were therefore durably enqueued onto a queue that never runs them, and the failure is silent because the row simply stays `AVAILABLE`. Routing is now an exact-match table over the dispatchable job types, unknown types are quarantined on `maintenance` rather than guessed at, and a new `apps/api` test asserts that the routing table and `createProductionAutomationJobHandlers()` cover exactly the same type set so the two halves of the contract cannot drift again. The previous queue test never caught this because it asserted routing only against invented type names (`ANALYZE_MATCH`, `APPLICATION_FILL`, `RESUME_UPLOAD`) that exist nowhere in the system; it now uses the real types. Evidence: 22/22 queue/routing tests pass; full suite 777 passed / 0 failed.
831. G19 authorization-matrix coverage closed for the document endpoints: the four authenticated document routes previously had no test proving that `router.use(authenticate)` actually guards them, and the download route's fail-closed row filter (`scanStatus: 'CLEAN'`, `deletedAt: null`, and the not-expired `OR` clause) was asserted only indirectly. Added 8 tests covering unauthenticated rejection on all four endpoints with no storage or metadata side effect, the exact scoping of the download and delete lookups, 404 for quarantined/deleted/expired objects, cross-tenant delete refusal with no storage call and no tombstone, approval attribution to the authenticated caller rather than client-supplied `approvedBy`, and rejection of an upload bound to another tenant's application before any storage write.

837. Regression I introduced and then fixed, recorded because it changes the meaning of the routing fix in entry 830: making job routing exact-match broke three integration fixtures that had silently depended on the old prefix behaviour. `APPLICATION_FIXTURE` (queue-worker), `APPLICATION_REDIS_OUTAGE_FIXTURE` (redis-outage), and `APPLICATION_QUEUE_STALL_FIXTURE` (queue-stall) all matched the retired `APPLICATION` prefix and reached the `applications` queue by accident; under exact-match they were quarantined on `maintenance`, so the worker under test never received them. This surfaced only because entry 835 wired the never-run stall test into a runner. Fixed by giving each fixture a real routed type (`VERIFY_SUBMISSION_CONFIRMATION`) with a comment explaining that the test supplies its own handler, rather than by adding test-only names to the production routing table. I verified first that every production call site uses a type present in the routing table, so the exact-match table is complete for real traffic.
838. Queue stall/lock-loss recovery verified for the first time on 2026-09-15: `queue-stall.integration.test.ts` genuinely exercises real BullMQ lock loss (the lock key is deleted out from under a blocked handler), the `stalled` event, PostgreSQL lease expiry, `reconcileExpiredAutomationJobLeases` returning `{ available: 1, deadLetter: 0 }`, redispatch, and exactly one recovered success with `attemptCount: 2`, `deliveryGeneration: 2`, and cleared lease fields — while the stale completion is rejected. This is direct evidence for G29 beyond the previously recorded store-only claims. Runners verified after the change: database 32 files/123 tests, queue 3 files/16 tests, redis-outage 1 file/1 test, S3 1 file/3 tests, backup drill passed (305,356 bytes dumped, encrypted, checksummed, decrypted, restore-readable).
839. G18 acceptance closed on 2026-09-15. The goal was already marked VERIFIED, but the acceptance audit found the evidence did not support that status in two places, so both were fixed rather than re-asserted. (1) Real defect: `verificationFor` in `packages/job-engine/src/form-intelligence.ts` did `if (field.verification) return field.verification;`. `field.verification` is webpage-controlled snapshot data — `isApplicationFormField` only checks `id`, `name`, `label`, `kind`, and `required` — so any truthy string a hostile page attached to an annotated verification control became a typed `HumanVerificationKind`. It then flowed into `assessment.verification`, into the assessment `reason` string by interpolation, and out to `requestHumanVerification({ type })` in `apps/api/src/services/greenhouse-application.ts`, whose `human_verifications.type` column is an unbounded Postgres `String`. The disposition stayed `HUMAN_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED`/`REVIEW_ONLY`, so this was never a bypass — the safety property held — but untrusted text was reaching a typed field and durable verification evidence, which is the type-confusion boundary this project treats as untrusted input everywhere else. Fixed by adding `HUMAN_VERIFICATION_KINDS` and `isHumanVerificationKind` to `packages/security/src/browser-policy.ts` (the module that owns the type), validating at the choke point in `verificationFor`, and stripping an unrecognized declared kind at the extractor boundary so it cannot travel inside extracted field evidence. An unrecognized declaration does not disable detection: the deterministic regex fingerprint still runs, so a genuine `g-recaptcha-response` control is still caught. (2) Evidence gap: `SAFE` had zero test assertions and `HIGH_RISK` had one, so "policy classification is deterministic" for all seven classes was asserted rather than demonstrated. Added a table pinning all seven dispositions on exact inputs, an invariance test proving classification is unchanged by DOM control id and by field order, a `SAFE` reachability test showing the optional-checkbox path is the only route to it and is not a blanket exemption for other optional controls, tests that a page-declared verification kind is rejected and that a contracted one (`MFA`) is preserved, and an end-to-end test proving `SENSITIVE`/`HIGH_RISK` controls are never filled from profile data, never filled from an unapproved AI draft, filled only by an explicit owner-approved answer at the exact question identity, and not filled for a different owner. Mutation-checked the substantive ones: reverting `verificationFor` to the truthy check fails the rejection tests, and removing the profile-mapping order makes the high-risk fill test fail. Verified: form-intelligence 64 tests and browser-policy 15 tests pass (79 combined); the six-file focused G18 set passes 106 with 1 database-gated file skipped and no failures; the full configured suite rises from 785 to 799 passed with 132 skipped and 0 failed; the disposable PostgreSQL runner passes 32 files/123 tests; all workspace typechecks, root lint, the coverage-integrity audit, and `git diff --check` are clean. G18 acceptance criteria are now met with evidence: all seven dispositions deterministic and pinned, provider-neutral architecture unchanged, adapters still thin, hostile webpage input cannot name a verification type, and high-risk/sensitive answers still require explicit recorded human approval.
840. G19 acceptance closed on 2026-09-15, and the recording corrects an overstatement the previous evidence column carried. The row had claimed "full database cleanup/reproducibility remains incomplete" as an open gap without quantifying it; measuring it showed the suite was not merely incomplete but actively leaking on every run. A per-file probe over the 32-file database runner found eight leaking files adding, per run: 19 `jobs`, 2 `users`, 1 `applications`, 1 `resume_versions`, and 1 `outbox_events`. Cause in every case was the same shape — a fixture job row is created in `beforeAll` or a per-test helper, while `afterAll` deletes only the fixture *user*; jobs are not user-owned, so the cascade never reached them. `foundation.integration.test.ts` was worse: it inserted users, jobs, resume versions, applications, and outbox events by raw SQL with no cleanup at all, in tests whose whole purpose is proving that cross-tenant writes are rejected — so the successful inserts those checks depend on accumulated forever. `discovery-hardening.integration.test.ts` had two raw-SQL job inserts in one test that were tracked by neither the set-based cleanup nor anything else. Fixed by deleting each fixture job explicitly after the user cascade, tracking in-test fixtures in the same set the file already used, and giving the raw-SQL file an ordered `afterAll` that removes rows in reverse insertion order (discovery items and runs before the jobs they reference). No assertion was weakened and no test was skipped to make this pass; the suites still report 32 files/123 tests and 3 files/16 tests. Verified: a full database-suite run now leaves all 43 tables byte-identical, checked by a new gate. That gate, `scripts/verify-database-isolation.mjs` (`npm run test:database-isolation`), snapshots every table's row count, runs the suite, and exits non-zero if anything changed. Mutation-checked both directions: removing a single `job.deleteMany` from `email-outcomes.integration.test.ts` makes it report `jobs: 789 -> 790 (+1)` and exit 1, and restoring it returns to `OK: all 43 tables unchanged` and exit 0. It is deliberately not wired into CI, because CI runs with no database (the database runner is not a CI step either) and the gate would report GATED rather than a result. Everything else in the G19 acceptance list was re-verified against live local infrastructure rather than re-asserted: private bucket and object access, object metadata in PostgreSQL with binaries only in object storage, tenant and document ownership, immutable `ResumeVersion` linkage, SHA-256 checksums, extension/MIME agreement plus magic-byte validation including the DOCX container check, size limits, encryption references, signed access, retention, deletion, tamper detection, authorization, audit trail, duplicate/concurrent upload convergence, and the ClamAV integration boundary that fails closed as `SCAN_UNAVAILABLE` when the scanner is unconfigured or errors and as `MALWARE_DETECTED` when it reports an infection. The eight-condition selection contract was confirmed by reading `evidenceMatches` and the preflight in `provider-submission.ts`: tenant, user, application, exact ResumeVersion, approval status with matching approver, artifact kind, checksum shape, and object metadata are each checked before any bytes are read, and the browser worker receives only in-memory buffers through Playwright `setInputFiles` — no filesystem path is ever handed to the page. The only remaining G19 boundary is production S3/KMS/scanner infrastructure, which this project deliberately does not touch, so the goal is now VERIFIED against a local MinIO/PostgreSQL equivalent rather than marked complete on the strength of unit tests.
841. G16 chain integration closed on 2026-09-15, and it exposed a real defect that also blocked G17. The G16 acceptance chain (FORM → FIELD DETECTION → FIELD MAPPING → APPROVED ANSWERS → EXACT DOCUMENT SELECTION → DOCUMENT VALIDATION → DOCUMENT UPLOAD → MULTI-STEP → VALIDATION → HUMAN VERIFICATION → DURABLE EVIDENCE) was previously evidenced only per-link, never as one composition, so a new live-PostgreSQL test now drives all of it against a two-step form: a seeded owner-approved `ObjectMetadata` linked to the application's exact `ResumeVersion`, a seeded owner-approved answer keyed on the normalized question identity, a step 1 carrying a required profile field, a required resume `FILE` control and an approved custom question, and a step 2 carrying a challenge. It asserts each link in order — the four durable question identities with their policy classes, required state and per-step provenance, the exact fill calls, one `readAuthorized` with the stored checksum and `resumeVersionId`, exactly one `uploadDocument` carrying the stored checksum and in-memory bytes, exactly one `advance`, a `PENDING` `CAPTCHA` human verification, `WAITING_FOR_USER`, two `GREENHOUSE_FORM_STEP_ASSESSED` audit rows, the retained immutable object identity, and the persisted answers with owner-bound provenance. The defect the composition exposed: `ApplicationFormAdapter.fillCurrentStep` computes `advanced` at the moment the adapter finishes filling, but approved-document upload, cover-letter document attachment, and supplementary cover-letter text all run afterwards in the orchestration layer, because those systems are not part of the adapter. A step whose only remaining blocker was the resume `FILE` control therefore always reported `advanced: false`; the step loop broke there with `needsReview` false, the remaining steps were never inspected, and the application was written as `FORM_FILLED` after only its first step had been answered — and because `loadPreparedApplication` returns early for `FORM_FILLED`, a re-run would return `FORM_FILLED` again without looking at the rest of the form. In `provider-submission.ts` the same ordering was worse: the upload happens inside the loop and the very next check was `if (!fill.advanced) throw PRECONDITION_FAILED`, so no multi-step provider form whose non-final step carries a resume upload could ever be submitted. Fixed once, in the shared rule rather than per provider: `stepIsComplete` is now the single exported predicate for "this step may be left" (it was previously inlined in the adapter), and `advanceStepIfComplete` re-evaluates that same predicate on the post-fill result and advances only when nothing is still blocking. The Greenhouse/Lever orchestration calls it after all three post-fill systems; `provider-submission.ts` calls it after attaching the authorized documents, removing only the blockers its own uploads resolved, so a genuinely unresolved blocker still fails closed exactly as before. Two things found while fixing this and worth recording: `provider-submission.test.ts` replaced `@jobagent/job-engine` with a partial `vi.mock` factory, so the new export was `undefined` and eight tests failed with a wrapped `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME` that pointed at nothing — the factory now pulls the real `advanceStepIfComplete` through `importActual` rather than faking the rule the suite depends on; and my first version of the provider-submission change also rebuilt `filledFieldIds`, which no consumer reads — the fixtures caught it as `TypeError: fill.filledFieldIds is not iterable`, and the change was narrowed to the blocking set instead of making production code tolerant of a malformed adapter result. Evidence: the new chain test passes against live PostgreSQL (database runner 32 files/124 tests, up from 123, with the isolation gate reporting all 43 tables unchanged); `provider-submission` passes 20/20 including a new test that advances past a step whose only blocker was the resume upload this submission supplied, and one proving a blocker the submission could not supply still fails closed with no advance and no submit; real Chromium fixtures pass 16/16 Greenhouse and 9/9 Lever; job-engine form-intelligence passes 67/67 including three new cases for the deferred rule; the full configured suite rises from 799 to 804 passed with 133 skipped and 0 failed; queue 3 files/16 tests, S3 3/3, all workspace typechecks, root lint, and the coverage-integrity audit are clean. Mutation-checked in both directions: making `advanceStepIfComplete` a no-op fails the deferred-advance unit test, making `stepIsComplete` always true fails four tests including the fail-closed ones, and restoring `const stepResult = fill;` in `provider-submission.ts` fails exactly the new submission test.

The state graph and integration regression are covered; the focused state-graph test passes 4/4 and API typecheck/lint pass. The current PostgreSQL runner returned its explicit `GATED` result because the Docker daemon is unavailable, so the new live expiry/re-authorization assertion is not counted as passed.
